ORANGE ANNIVERSARY.

The anniversary of the Colonel Waring Lo ige of the Loyal Orange Institution of Great Britain was celebrated on Saturday at the Viaduct Hotel, Earlestown, when Bro. Tunstall, the host, placed a capital " spread" before the members and visiting brethren and friends who attended. At the meet- ing afterwards, Bro, G. Wright, W.M., olcupied the chair, and Bro. Pilling acted as vice-chairman. They were supported by Bro. J. Tunstall, of Liver- pool, and Bro. J. Robinson, of Haydock. The usual toasts and the toast of the Order were duly honoured, capital speeches being made by Bros. Tunstall and Robinson. During the evening the Apollo Glee Party gave "Comrades in Arms" and "The Sailors' Corps." Bro. Monks sang " Love's Request," which was followed by " Dear England," contributed by Bro. Wright. "Queen of the Earth" was given by Bro. C. It. Lea, and Bro. Sephton rendered "Ever of Thee." A recitation entitled" Man was made to mourn" was given by Bro. Sutton. Bros. Pilling (Ashton) and T. George also contributed items,

EARLESTOWN CO OPERATIVE SOMETT. The 152nd quarterly meeting of this society was held in the Co-operative Hall, on Monday evening. Mr. D. Forrester presided, and there was a large attendance of members. The report and balance sheet which were submitted and adopted snowed the cash receipts for the quarter to be £6,378 178 being a decrease on last quarter of .£42 108 2td There was no diminished trade represented by a ' small reduction in receipts under last quarter. The net profits realised were £1,024 7s. 11d., which it was propoled to distribute by paying a dividend of 3e in the .£ to members, which would require £046 19s. .£33 10s was taken for special painting and renovating account, and the balance .£.38 18311 d was added to the contingent fund: During the . . . .

quarter 62 new members had been admitted, find 16 had withdrawn, leaving 1,265 on the books, 919 of which had brought in cheques. The Chairman in a few introductory remarks commented upon the satisfactory condition of the society, and the suc- cessful working of the branch shops which had been opened, and which had been the means

of bringing to the society an increased membership. There were six nominations for the tvvo vacancies on the Committee of Management, and the voting was as follows: J. G. Mullins 107, P. Parr 87, G. Bailey 33. L. A. Bullock 30, Edward Adwards 13, John Limond 14. Messrs. Mullins and Parr were therefore re-elected. Mr, D. Forrester , was nominated for election as president. Tbe ques- tion of bat aking was introduced, and it was de- cided that instead of appointing additional stock- takers for the branch shops, to arrange for the em- ployees to take sto* under the supervision of a member of the Committee, and that the bo3lis be sent to the Wholesale Society for extension and calculation. The President said that the opening of the branch shops would be celebrated by a tea- party to be held in February next. A grant of s„5 was ordered to be made to the Technical Insitute Committee. A grant of .E 1 was also made to the Orchestral Society. The question Of making a grant to certain hospitals was broached and after a short discussion it was decided that the matter, be Con- sidered by the Committee and again brought before the members at the next quarterly meeting. NOTES BY THE. ifir,Jr AT E kitLESTOWN.

(BY A CORRESPONDENT)

lam pleased to learn that Mr. John Ball, who has during the past week or so,been lying ssrionsly ill, is now in a fair way to renvery. Thera have been many enquiriss trom numerous friends and acquaintances, out as his medicil attendant forbade anybody to see him, they will doubtless to.i relieved at this intimation.

The Old Folks' Social evening last week was an unqualified success in every way. There were nearly 300 old people over 60 years of age present to the tea, and the sight of such a gathering of the aged was inspiring. The oldest woman present was 97 years of age,ani the oldest man was 95 and both of them hope to be spared to he present at another similar gathering next year. The old lady could not be prevailed upon to honour the company with song,but the old gentleman willingly volunteered and it was affecting to see the tottering old man in front of 300 expectant faees. the song was' Tally Ho ! "and con-ilering his great age, he sang it re- markably well, the other old people singing the chorus with much gusto.

The Bird and Pourry show held on Saturday in connection with the Earles•owa Fanciers' Associa- tion was a gigantic success, so fir as the entries and exhibits are concerned, ant the Committee have every reason to be gratified at the result of their labours. They expected a big “ttendance, but they were quite unprepared to deal with the large num- ber who put in an appearance. The exhibits were of a very high ea's, alai 801313 of the birds had gained first prizes at the principal shows in the kingdom: There were some very peculiar foreign ducks shown by a gentleman from Birkenhead, which were well worth seeing. A good deal of in- terest centred round a nightingale slown amoagst the British birds, owing no doubt to the extremely rare sight of one of this species in a cage. The Pigeons claimed a lot of attention, and there were some very fine specimens amongst the fancy class. The show no doubt encourages a spirit of emulation which in its turn brings forth a taste for rearing poultry of a high class kind.

===== 0002601_18981125_art0136.txt | included=true | included =====
FOOTBALL.

Earlestown have managed by a very narrow shave to slip into the first round proper of the Lan- cashire Junior Cup Competition. During the first part of the game they held Ashton well in hand, and obtained a clear lead, which they ought never to have lost. In the second half, however, the quality of the play on the home side distinetly fell away, whilst that of Ashton materially improved,so much so that they certainly lnoked every inch win- ners. They did manage to equalise, and at the call of time it WWI 3 all. The referee arringed for an extra half hour, and just before the first quarter was up Earlestown put on what beqame the win- ning pint. From then until the end of the game, Earlestown played on the defensive, and Ashton were unable to break through.

Ashton have protestei, first ',lonise of darkness. and secondly, they allegs thst Limond who played full back, was not eligibla.•Ths first complaint was easily squashed on the information of the referee, and the second will receive a similar fate, as there is abundant proof of Limond's eligibility, as he is a qualifying playing member of the team, having played for Earlestown last season. •

After such a close fiaish with Ashton, Earlestown visited Bamfurlong last Saturlay, and were very near sucminbing to the Collier,. They were at a disadvantage early in the game, two of their players being hurt. AU things cansidered they did well to secure a draw, and thus they ad I another point to those alrealy obGained.

This week there is another radical champ in the team, and judging by its composition it should cer- tainly be an improvement in strength. "Cap' Les has been removed from centre tor ward to rigot wing in partnership with Smith, azia. a new player "Csrlin," with a good reputation, tikes the vacancy at centre. Adlington are visitors to Earlestown on Saturday, and an easy victory should accrue to the home eleven.

Newton had as visitors on Saturday. Seacombe Swifts, and it is a matter of surprise that the game ended in a draw. Toe Swifts have a good reputa- tion. and Newton had misgivings as to the result. Their combination, however, was an improvement on previous games, and by dint of hard work, coupled with excellent goal keeping by Ellara, tar)), succeeded in keeping on level terms with the Swifts.

Ths accident to the Warrington St. Mary's goal keeper, during the y.lanie with Nawton Athletic, was a most unfortunate occurrence. Players cannot be too caraful, and no word would b 3 too strong in condemnation of tha "going for" tactics which are becoming 83 prevalent, amongst junior talons. Aspinall has bean in a dangerous condition, bat I ' am thankful to say that he is now improving, and goo i hopes ara eulertained tor his ultimdta recovery.

What the wrong was had been well known tor a long time to those Guardians wno had taken an active interest in the management of the institution. From time to time statements had been made reflecting upon the manage- ment of the institution, and c astiag blame on those res- ponsible. Se hoped there would be no recrimination of that kind in the discu,sion of this matter. A certain proportion of the blame rested on the Guardians them- selves,not the present Guardians, but their predecessors. They had delayed the erection of the new hospital for an unreasonable time. It was impossible to conduct the work of the hospital with the building and staff they bad at present. It had been most difficult to get work done with any approach to decency or in anything like a satisfactory manner. That was notorious. Now the Board had proeeeded with the • erection of a new hospital, and when that was complete a considerable part of the difficulty would be cleared oway. In the meantimethat difficulty had been rendered more acute by several other causes. As they knew, the Local Goverament Board bad insis- ted on an alteration in the nursing system all over the country. That had led to the increased demand for trained nurses. There they could not offer decent ac- commodation, either for the sick poor or for teose paid to nurse them. They cculcl not wonder that women with a decent education, and who had been properly trained, telt a great discomfort at having to work in an institution like that. That had been a source of a great deal of trouble. The superintendent nurse had tried to do her bests but she had beea hampered in every way, and there had been constant sources of friction and trouble. It was inevltable that the Master and Matron should be brought into cleflict occas•ciaatly, with the head of the nursing staff. There had- been, a certain amount of friction, He did not blame either the Master and Matron or the superintendent nurse. Such friction as had arisen was inevitalelo.

Mr. KsowLEs : What did the nurse complain about ? Mr. A. J. JOLLEY said fiele Stated in &AAR what he had summarised. Th^re was also a complaint from her abut the treatment she had received from one of the msmtv,rs of the Ladle? Committee.

Mr. KNWLES : lies that member resigned ? Mr. A. J. JOLLEY tad she had. On behalf of the superintendent it was only fair to add that she had emphasized the fact that the member had apologized, and as far as that pa,t,cular incident was discerned It was at an ead. That was not tie cause of har resignation. After further discussion the minutes were approved.

NOTES ON GAMES AND ATHLETICS.

RUGBY FOOTBALL.

Now shall I begin ? I know not, but I must break the news gently. On Saturday Warrington were beaten by 35 points to nil, and at the same time outclassed, annihilated, demoralised and pulverised by Oldham'. There ! that's gentle enough for anyone, and cannot even hurt the feelings of the Committee. I might have blurted it out brutally, but the news ii too, too sad to be told an unprepared listener. Sudden shocks have been known to kill, as, for ins`alce, that felt by the man who tumbled horn the thirteenth storey of a New Yoik tenement.
